## Explicit model defect claims (subject to independent verification)

### When Config.RestoreCommittedLogs is enabled, does NewRaft recover the membership configuration that the node's own log already contains, and what does the recovered node then do with the configuration-dependent decisions (election eligibility and quorum membership) that follow?

Current disposition: active.
Question `98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-question-0` v1; sources: src_newraft_startup, src_restore_committed, src_process_config_entry, src_follower_election_guard ([full references](state.json)).
Current model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification).

Current interpretation `8afcaa2327d94db1a6720eae0efbd4e3-interpretation-1` · declared predicate does not hold: Correction note: an earlier statement in this account described processConfigurationLogEntry as the only writer of the tracked configuration state, which is too strong - restoreSnapshot installs the newest local snapshot's committed configuration (api.go:677-678), installSnapshot installs the metadata of a streamed snapshot (raft.go:1960-1961), appendConfigurationEntry records the configuration it appends on the leader (raft.go:1238), and the conflict path in appendEntries rolls the latest configuration back to the committed one (raft.go:1548) - while processConfigurationLogEntry remains the writer for entries taken from the log, called from appendEntries and from the startup scan. The mechanism the account relies on is unchanged and in fact sharpened: the startup inputs are the snapshot's configuration and the log scan, and the scan's lower bound is what the feature moves. Extension note: this successor cites the repeat of the two-voter probe (receipt 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195), which returns the same split as the first execution, so the boundary that a node recovering without a configuration still votes rests on two runs; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the two-voter probe (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1), which shows that a node recovering without a configuration still grants a vote to a healthy peer while never becoming a candidate itself; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the revised authority experiment (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357), whose harness keeps committing until the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index before restarting the node; the witness reappeared under that explicit premise and the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor reports the repeat of the multi-node experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1), which reproduced the commit-rule, durable-snapshot and availability cases with identical operands but not the authority witness, because the caller's persisted commit index was zero in that execution and the startup scan therefore recovered the configuration; the authority paragraph now states that precondition and the counterevidence records it. A revised experiment that holds the precondition explicitly is submitted with this batch. Extension note: this successor adds receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, which observes in a three-voter group that a node recovering without a configuration never attempts candidacy while the same node and log with the replay disabled do; the sources, the three earlier executions, the knowledge uses, the conclusion and the remaining counterevidence are otherwise unchanged, and the counter about the observation window is strengthened with the new control's sample counts. Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only by a transcribed latency; the control's election is now stated as the receipt records it (19 polls at a 5ms interval, about 95ms), not from an earlier construction run. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence and limitations - is unchanged. Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Authority attempt. Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). A node whose tracked configuration is empty therefore does not merely fail to win an election: it never attempts to acquire authority, so it cannot be the source of a new leader even when the surviving members could form a quorum with it. The claim is bounded on the other side by a two-voter probe: receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 shows the same recovered node tracking no configuration (event 19: latest_config_index 0, no members, with the group size recorded as two) and, when its peer is restarted so that it must campaign, the peer is elected - because a majority of two is both members, that leadership required the defective node's vote - while the defective node again showed zero candidate samples in the sampling window (event 20, comparison holds). The defect therefore removes the node as a candidate, not as a voter: it can still support a peer's candidacy, and its harm is that it cannot itself be the source of a new leader. The precondition is visible in the same operand: the empty configuration appears when the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, because that index becomes lastApplied and pushes the scan above the log's configuration entry. The revised experiment holds the premise explicitly and the witness reappeared: keeping the leader committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, A again tracked no configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4, with no extra applies needed) and again never entered Candidate in the sampling window while the control that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 257 samples (events 16 and 18, receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, comparisons violated and holds). That receipt also ran the other three cases a further time with the same operands (events 4, 6 to 8, 11 and 13). The repeat of the earlier version of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target. The two-voter probe has now been executed twice from the same retained inputs: in both runs the recovered node tracks no configuration (staged commit index 5, latest_config_index 0, no members), shows zero candidate samples while its peer campaigns, and the peer reaches Leader in a group of two, which requires that node's vote (receipts b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 and 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195, events 19 and 20 in each).

Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the observed consequences are a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain and a durable record that re-imposes the loss.; The commit-rule violation needs the removed server C to stay reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted; if it were stopped or unreachable the same stale configuration would instead leave the node unable to commit. The receipt therefore shows one reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; One could argue the snapshot in the durable-state case is only as good as the state the node tracks, and that a caller should not snapshot a node whose configuration is empty. The counter is that the node is an ordinary cluster member whose own log contains the configuration entries, nothing in the API or the documentation marks its snapshots as unusable, and the resulting metadata then defeats a restart that has the feature disabled, so the corruption outlives the condition that created it.; The flag-off restart's failure to lead could be attributed to the 1500ms observation window rather than to the empty configuration. The counter is the source guard that refuses elections while configurations.latestIndex is zero (raft.go:225-241) plus two controls inside the same window: the single-voter control elected itself in 19 polls of the harness's 5ms interval (receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c, event 6), and in the authority case the node that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples while the node with the empty configuration showed zero candidate samples (receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, events 16 and 18).; The harness assigns roles deliberately (A restarts, B is partitioned, leadership is moved with the library's transfer) and it takes the snapshot explicitly rather than through the automatic cadence. Those choices select the scenario but do not supply the stale or empty configuration, which event 6 shows against the node's own log, nor the acknowledgement, which C's log carries.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469) and is retained as a harness artifact; the later receipts contain the same scenario with both defects exercised.; The authority case removes the other voters by shutting them down, so it shows that the defective node never attempts candidacy; it does not show an outage that a correct recovery would have prevented, because the recovered three-voter configuration also lacks a quorum with one voter left. What the receipt adds is the capability difference, not a counterfactual outage.; The authority witness depends on a property of the caller's store: A's persisted commit index must be non-zero, because that value becomes lastApplied and pushes the startup scan above the log's configuration entry. The repeat shows the other side of that condition - with a persisted commit index of 0 the scan starts at index 1, A recovers its configuration and does attempt candidacy (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1, events 15 and 16). The claim is therefore bounded to restarts whose store reports a non-zero commit index: the revised experiment establishes that premise explicitly and the witness reappears (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, staged commit index 4 with no extra applies needed, zero candidate samples), while the repeat that lacked the premise shows the other side. Both valid executions therefore share the same precondition, which their staged_commit_index operands make inspectable.; The authority and configuration claims must not be read as the node leaving the cluster: in a two-voter group the recovered node with an empty tracked configuration still voted, so its peer was elected with a majority of two (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1, events 19 and 20). The node's limitation is candidacy rather than participation, and any consequence that assumes it is absent from every future majority would overstate the effect.; The two-voter vote result is reproduced rather than sampled once, but both runs use the same harness, the same two-voter shape and the same 3-second window; a peer whose log were behind the recovered node's would exercise a different branch of the vote checks, which is not covered.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process and the harness uses a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified, and no production store or snapshot store is exercised.; The durable-state case snapshots a node that has no snapshot on its stores and then restarts with the replay disabled; shipping such a snapshot to a follower, or corruption arising from the automatic snapshot cadence, is not exercised, and deleting the corrupt snapshot is not tested as a recovery.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one committed removal, keeps the removed server running, and injects no message loss, reordering or crash, so the decision defect is observed under a clean transport.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node scenario appears in two receipts with the same operands, the durable-state scenario in one, and the observation windows are 1500ms for non-participation and 2000ms apply timeouts.; The authority witness now has two valid executions and one repeat in which its premise did not hold; both valid executions report the same precondition operand (a staged commit index of 4) and the second was produced by a harness that enforces it, so the remaining gap is only that the precondition itself comes from the caller's store behaviour rather than from anything the test controls end to end.
Hypothesis: restoreFromCommittedLogs sets lastApplied to the persisted commit index before the startup configuration scan, so the scan begins above every configuration entry at an index <= that commit index. The node keeps the snapshot's committed configuration, or an empty configuration when it has no snapshot, even though its log holds newer committed configuration entries. Because that configuration state decides election eligibility, quorumSize, and the membership used by the leader's commitment, the recovery can leave a node unable to participate, and it can leave a node that can win an election counting quorum from a stale member set.
Registered discriminator: Restart two identically built real prefixes on top of the same stores: one with RestoreCommittedLogs on and one with it off. Compare the recovered configuration (index, voter/nonvoter membership) against the newest configuration entry in the node's own log, and whether the node reaches leadership within a fixed window.

Fixed experiment `98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-experiment` v1: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Restart a single-voter raft node on top of its own retained stores, once with RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and once with it disabled, on two prefix shapes: a prefix with no snapshot, and a prefix whose snapshot precedes a committed configuration change. Report the recovered configuration (index, membership) beside the newest configuration entry in the node's own log, and whether the restarted node reaches leadership inside a 1.5s window (30 election/heartbeat timeouts at 50ms).
History / supplied conditions: Each case calls assuranceBuildPrefix: NewInmemTransport with a fresh random address; inmemConfig with LocalID = 'server-' + address and RestoreCommittedLogs = true; NewInmemCommitTrackingStore, NewInmemStore and NewInmemSnapshotStore; BootstrapCluster with a single Voter server at that address; NewRaft with a fresh MockFSM. The prefix then waits for real leadership through the normal election path, applies four LogCommand entries, and for the snapshot cases takes a real Snapshot, then issues AddNonvoter for a peer that never connects (committed by the single voter) and applies two more commands. GetCommitIndex, LastIndex and a per-index log scan record the persisted commit index and every configuration entry with its index and membership, then Shutdown closes the node. The case then creates a new transport bound to the same address, copies the config with RestoreCommittedLogs set to the case value, and calls NewRaft with the same log store, stable store, snapshot store and a fresh MockFSM. It polls State() every 5ms for 1500ms, then asks the main thread for the tracked configurations through r.configurationsCh (the same channel takeSnapshot uses) and reads snapshot index, AppliedIndex, CommitIndex and LastIndex.
Assumptions: The prefix runs with RestoreCommittedLogs enabled so that the commit index is staged into the commit-tracking log store; otherwise a restart with the feature enabled reads 0 and the scan start would equal the snapshot index.; The restarted node keeps the same address and ID as the prefix, so the configuration recorded in its log and snapshot still refers to it.; The FSM is an in-memory MockFSM and a fresh instance is used for each restart, which is the mode the feature targets; the FSM state is therefore rebuilt from the snapshot plus the replayed committed logs.; InmemCommitTrackingStore is a test facility whose StageCommitIndex does not share a write with StoreLogs; the probe uses it only as the store that exposes a persisted commit index and does not claim its durability properties.
Excluded scope: No multi-node cluster, partition, or crash of a leader is exercised, so the commit-loss consequence of a stale member set is not measured here.; No snapshot-on-start or FSM-implemented ConfigurationStore variant is exercised.; No production log store (for example a bolt-backed store) is exercised; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified.
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Fixed experiment `6b30dd76d4b64190be2808a0e7abb041-experiment` v1: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/6b30dd76d4b64190be2808a0e7abb041-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Three in-memory voters A, B and C on one another's transports. A snapshots while {A,B,C} is committed; the leader then removes C, which commits against {A,B}, and commits three more entries. A is shut down and restarted with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled over the same stores, while B is disconnected from A and C. The harness reports A's tracked configuration, whether A wins the election, and for a command applied after that: the client result, A's commit and applied index, whether A's FSM applied the payload, and whether the entry is present in A's, B's and C's logs.
History / supplied conditions: The harness builds three assuranceMultiNode values: NewInmemTransport with a random address each, inmemConfig with LocalID = 'server-' + address and RestoreCommittedLogs true for every node, plus NewInmemCommitTrackingStore, NewInmemStore and NewInmemSnapshotStore per node. All transports are connected pairwise; BootstrapCluster is called on each node with the same three-voter configuration; NewRaft is called for each with a fresh MockFSM. The harness then waits for a real leader, applies one command through it, and takes a user snapshot on A through A.raft.Snapshot() while {A,B,C} is committed. It calls RemoveServer(C) on the leader and waits for that future, then applies three more commands, and records A's staged commit index, last index and the newest configuration entry in A's log. A is shut down and B is disconnected from A's transport and C's transport; a new transport bound to A's old address is created and connected to C only. NewRaft is called again on A's stores with RestoreCommittedLogs true and a fresh MockFSM. The harness reads A's tracked configurations through r.configurationsCh, waits up to five seconds for A to lead, and if it does, applies the payload 'stale-quorum-entry' with a two second timeout, then samples A's log entry term, the same index in B's and C's logs, A's commit and applied indexes, and A's FSM log contents.
Assumptions: The removed server C keeps running and keeps answering AppendEntries, which is what a removal without shutting the process down leaves in place; the harness does not simulate C being killed.; A's snapshot is taken through the normal snapshot goroutine while the committed configuration is still {A,B,C}, so its configuration index is the bootstrap entry's index and its configuration contains C.; B is partitioned before A is restarted, so the committed configuration's only other voter cannot contribute an acknowledgement.; The nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process; leadership, terms and quorum arithmetic are the real code paths.
Excluded scope: No process-level restart, no production log store, and no attempt to complete the downstream loss of the entry; the predicate stops at the commitment rule.; No case in which the restarted node is the only stale voter or in which the stale configuration excludes a voter rather than including a removed one.
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Fixed experiment `bf92fdb38535413ca18d171812c22f8b-experiment` v2: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/bf92fdb38535413ca18d171812c22f8b-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Three in-memory voters A, B and C on one another's transports. A snapshots while {A,B,C} is committed; the leader then removes C, which commits against {A,B}, and commits three more entries. A is shut down and restarted with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled over the same stores, while B is disconnected from A and C. The harness reports A's tracked configuration, whether A wins the election, and for a command applied after that: the client result, A's commit and applied index, whether A's FSM applied the payload, and whether the entry is present in A's, B's and C's logs.
History / supplied conditions: The harness builds three assuranceMultiNode values: NewInmemTransport with a random address each, inmemConfig with LocalID = 'server-' + address and RestoreCommittedLogs true for every node, plus NewInmemCommitTrackingStore, NewInmemStore and NewInmemSnapshotStore per node. All transports are connected pairwise; BootstrapCluster is called on each node with the same three-voter configuration; NewRaft is called for each with a fresh MockFSM. The harness then waits for a real leader, applies one command through it, and takes a user snapshot on A through A.raft.Snapshot() while {A,B,C} is committed. It calls RemoveServer(C) on the leader and waits for that future, then applies three more commands, and records A's staged commit index, last index and the newest configuration entry in A's log. A is shut down and B is disconnected from A's transport and C's transport; a new transport bound to A's old address is created and connected to C only. NewRaft is called again on A's stores with RestoreCommittedLogs true and a fresh MockFSM. The harness reads A's tracked configurations through r.configurationsCh, waits up to five seconds for A to lead, and if it does, applies the payload 'stale-quorum-entry' with a two second timeout, then samples A's log entry term, the same index in B's and C's logs, A's commit and applied indexes, and A's FSM log contents.
Assumptions: The removed server C keeps running and keeps answering AppendEntries, which is what a removal without shutting the process down leaves in place; the harness does not simulate C being killed.; A's snapshot is taken through the normal snapshot goroutine while the committed configuration is still {A,B,C}, so its configuration index is the bootstrap entry's index and its configuration contains C.; B is partitioned before A is restarted, so the committed configuration's only other voter cannot contribute an acknowledgement.; The nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process; leadership, terms and quorum arithmetic are the real code paths.
Excluded scope: No process-level restart, no production log store, and no attempt to complete the downstream loss of the entry; the predicate stops at the commitment rule.; No case in which the restarted node is the only stale voter or in which the stale configuration excludes a voter rather than including a removed one.

Fixed experiment `e080feeb9663426b82b2c8cd316eaec0-experiment` v1: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/e080feeb9663426b82b2c8cd316eaec0-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Three in-memory voters A, B and C. A command is committed, A is shut down and restarted with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and no snapshot on its stores, and the harness records A's tracked configuration and the newest configuration entry in A's log. A command is committed again so A's FSM has something to snapshot, A takes a snapshot through the normal path, and the harness compares the snapshot's recorded configuration and configuration index with the configuration entry in A's log. A is then shut down and restarted again with the replay disabled, and the harness records the configuration the node comes up with.
History / supplied conditions: The harness builds three assuranceMultiNode values (random in-memory transport addresses, inmemConfig with RestoreCommittedLogs true, NewInmemCommitTrackingStore, NewInmemStore and NewInmemSnapshotStore per node), wires the transports pairwise, calls BootstrapCluster with the same three-voter configuration on each node and NewRaft with a fresh MockFSM each. It waits for a real leader, and if A won, moves leadership to B with LeadershipTransferToServer so that A can be restarted without stopping the cluster's leader. One command is committed and the harness waits until A's FSM has applied a command entry. A is shut down and a new transport on A's address is connected to B and C; NewRaft is called on A's stores with RestoreCommittedLogs true and a fresh MockFSM, and the harness records the tracked configurations through r.configurationsCh plus A's staged commit index, last index and newest configuration entry. One more command is committed and awaited in A's FSM, then A.Snapshot() is called and the harness reads the newest SnapshotMeta from A's snapshot store, comparing its Configuration and ConfigurationIndex with the log's configuration entry. A is shut down again, a further transport is created on the same address and connected to B and C, and NewRaft is called with RestoreCommittedLogs false; the harness records the restored snapshot index, the tracked configuration, the leader observation over 1500ms and the final state.
Assumptions: A's stores hold no snapshot before the first restart, so the flag-on startup replays the committed log above index 0 and the scan is skipped.; The snapshot is taken through the library's normal snapshot path (user snapshot request handled by the snapshot goroutine), so nothing in the harness supplies the configuration it records.; The second restart uses the same stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs false and a fresh in-memory FSM, so the configuration can only come from the snapshot metadata and the log scan.; The flag-off restart is expected to run without a leader in the observation window because an empty configuration prevents the node from starting an election.
Excluded scope: No multi-node partition and no commit-rule consequence in this case; the earlier multi-node experiment covers that.; No production snapshot store, no process-level restart, and no case in which a later configuration entry above the snapshot index repairs the tracked configuration.; The harness does not attempt to remove the corrupt snapshot, so recovery by deleting durable state is not measured.
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Fixed experiment `7db8c9fb0d794b08892b9349c54fa466-experiment` v1: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/7db8c9fb0d794b08892b9349c54fa466-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Three in-memory voters A, B and C. A leader is chosen, three commands are committed so A's staged commit index is non-zero, A is stopped and restarted over its own stores in one case with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and in the other with it disabled, and the harness records A's tracked configuration. The other two voters are then shut down, and the harness samples A's state every 5ms for 1500ms, reporting how often it was a follower, a candidate or a leader.
History / supplied conditions: For each case the harness builds three assuranceMultiNode values (random in-memory transport addresses, inmemConfig with RestoreCommittedLogs true and a fresh MockFSM per node), wires the transports pairwise, calls BootstrapCluster with the same three-voter configuration on each and NewRaft for each. It waits for a real leader and commits three commands through it, then records A's staged commit index and shuts A down. A new transport bound to A's address is connected to B and C, and NewRaft is called on A's stores with a fresh MockFSM and the case's RestoreCommittedLogs value; the harness reads A's tracked configurations through r.configurationsCh and reports the latest index, the voter count and the member ids. It then shuts B and C down, samples A's state every 5ms for 1500ms, and reports the follower, candidate and leader sample counts together with the last snapshot index and the final state.
Assumptions: The cluster configuration is stable, so no new configuration entry reaches A after its restart and its tracked configuration can only come from its own startup path.; A's stores hold no snapshot, so with the replay enabled the startup scan is skipped and A ends up with an empty tracked configuration; with the replay disabled the scan covers the log and A recovers the three-voter configuration.; Removing the other voters by shutting them down tests whether the surviving voter attempts candidacy, not whether it can win; with the recovered three-voter configuration A cannot win either, since its quorum is two.; The two cases use separate clusters built the same way, so the flag is the only difference.
Excluded scope: No assertion about winning an election or about cluster availability; the observation is the candidate attempt itself.; No membership change after the restart, no partitioned-but-live peer, and no production store or process-level restart.
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Fixed experiment `8dd0e551241244af988706dc6380ba58-experiment` v2: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/8dd0e551241244af988706dc6380ba58-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: Same four cases as the predecessor harness with one change: before A is restarted in the authority case the harness commits further commands through the leader until A's commit-tracking store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, then restarts A and samples its state as before. The extra applies are reported as premise_filler_applies.
History / supplied conditions: For each case the harness builds three assuranceMultiNode values, wires them, bootstraps the three-voter configuration and starts each node with a fresh MockFSM. It commits three commands through the elected leader, then keeps committing until A's commit-tracking store reports a non-zero commit index (at most twenty further applies, reported as premise_filler_applies), records that index and shuts A down. A new transport on A's address is connected to B and C, NewRaft is called on A's stores with a fresh MockFSM and the case's replay setting, and the harness reports A's tracked configuration. B and C are then shut down and A's state is sampled every 5ms for 1500ms, reporting follower, candidate and leader sample counts, whether candidacy was attempted and the final state.
Assumptions: A's persisted commit index is what restoreFromCommittedLogs reads at startup and it advances when A's log store stages a write, so more leader commits make it non-zero.; The premise loop is bounded and the case fails loudly if twenty further applies do not raise the value.; The rest of the authority case is unchanged: A restarts over its own stores with the case's replay setting, the other voters are shut down, and A's state is sampled every 5ms for 1500ms.
Excluded scope: No change to the other three cases in the harness.; No assertion about winning an election; the observation remains the candidate attempt.
Predecessor `7db8c9fb0d794b08892b9349c54fa466-experiment`; revision reason: The repeat showed that the authority case's premise is not guaranteed: A's persisted commit index can be zero at restart, in which case the startup scan recovers the configuration and the witness conditions do not arise. The revised harness keeps committing through the leader until A's store reports a non-zero commit index before restarting A, and reports how many extra applies that took.; actual changes: harness, source_ids, scope, prefix, responsibility, comparisons
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Fixed experiment `341e128828854db2a05ee7e8e8a98bf6-experiment` v1: [saved basis](fixed-experiments/341e128828854db2a05ee7e8e8a98bf6-experiment/basis.json).
Scope: A two-voter group A and B, where a majority is both members. B is made leader and three commands are committed, then the harness keeps committing until A's commit-tracking store reports a non-zero persisted commit index. A is stopped and restarted with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled, so it tracks no configuration, and the harness records that. B is then stopped and restarted as well, so it must campaign from a follower state and can only win with A's vote; the harness samples A's state while B campaigns and reports whether B was elected.
History / supplied conditions: The harness builds two assuranceMultiNode values, wires their in-memory transports, bootstraps the same two-voter configuration on each and starts both with a fresh MockFSM. It waits for a real leader and, if A won, transfers leadership to B with LeadershipTransferToServer. Three commands are committed through B and the harness keeps committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, then records that index and the number of extra applies. A is shut down, a new transport bound to A's address is connected to B, and NewRaft is called on A's stores with a fresh MockFSM and RestoreCommittedLogs enabled; the harness reads A's tracked configuration and emits it with the group size. B is then shut down too, a further transport bound to B's address is created and connected to A's, and NewRaft is called on B's stores with a fresh MockFSM and the replay disabled. While B campaigns, the harness samples A's state every 5ms for up to three seconds, stopping if B reaches Leader, and reports the sample counts and B's final state.
Assumptions: With two voters a majority is two, so B's election requires a grant from A in addition to its own vote; B's leadership is therefore evidence that A voted, which the receipt's group_voters operand makes checkable.; A's persisted commit index must be non-zero for the empty configuration to arise, and the harness keeps committing until it is, reporting how many extra applies that took.; Both nodes restart over their own stores on fresh transports bound to the same addresses, so the group keeps its two members.; The observation window for A's states is long enough for B to campaign: B's election timeout is the harness's 50ms and the window is three seconds.
Excluded scope: No case in which A is asked to vote for a candidate whose log is behind, and no membership change; the probe concerns whether a legitimate peer can still be elected.; No production store, process-level restart or multi-node group beyond the two voters.
Declared requirement / prediction: No fixed requirement; observations remain available for sourced interpretation.

Interpretation history:


Historical interpretation `98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-interpretation-0` · source explanation; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Source trace. NewRaft restores the newest usable snapshot, then, only with RestoreCommittedLogs, reads the persisted commit index from the commit-tracking log store, stores it and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754); processLogs sets lastApplied to that index (raft.go:1296-1364). Only after that does NewRaft run the scan that rebuilds the node's configuration from the log, and that scan starts at max(lastSnapshotIndex, lastApplied) + 1 (api.go:602-621). With the feature off, lastApplied equals the snapshot index, so the scan covers every configuration entry in the log; with the feature on, lastApplied is the commit index, which for a node that committed entries above its newest snapshot is strictly larger, so every configuration entry at an index <= that commit index is skipped even though it is present in the node's log and was applied to the FSM. The skipped entries can only have been processed by processConfigurationLogEntry, the sole writer of the tracked configuration state, and its startup caller is exactly that scan; FSM-side configuration storage does not feed back into r.configurations (raft.go:1605-1624). The resulting configuration state is not inert: configurations.latestIndex == 0 makes a follower refuse to start an election (raft.go:225-241), and configurations.latest supplies quorumSize, the voter set counted by checkLeaderLease, the membership map used by the leader's commitment, and the inConfiguration/hasVote admission checks for vote grants (raft.go:456-467, 1085-1094; commitment.go:35-64). Two consequences follow directly: a node with no snapshot recovers an empty configuration and can never elect itself (no reachable producer of AppendEntries or configuration entries exists in a single-voter cluster), and a node with a snapshot recovers the snapshot's older membership, including a member set that omits an entry its log already committed. The existing test for this feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration is identical to the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this case. The defect is scoped to deployments that enable RestoreCommittedLogs and use a commit-tracking log store; the intended duty is that the node recover the configuration its own log establishes, which the scan exists to do.

Sources: [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json), [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_follower_election_guard](state.json), [src_commitment_from_config](state.json), [src_quorum_size](state.json), [src_existing_restore_test](state.json), [src_try_stage_commit_index](state.json), [src_append_entries_staging](state.json), [src_restore_committed_docs](state.json); execution evidence: No execution receipt explicitly cited by this interpretation.
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) describes replaying committed logs to bring the FSM up to date and says nothing about configuration recovery, so one could argue that a stale configuration view is an unnamed side effect rather than a broken contract. The counter is that the scan at api.go:610-621 exists precisely to rebuild configuration state from the log at startup, and its start index is the only reason entries are skipped; no other path restores that state after the replay.; The skip also removes work: with RestoreCommittedLogs, configuration entries below the persisted commit index were already delivered to the FSM as commit tuples, so an FSM implementing ConfigurationStore has seen them. That does not restore r.configurations, which is raft's own decision input and is not derived from the FSM.
Interpretation limitations: The predicate recorded here is source-derived; the fixed experiment for this question has been submitted with this batch and its retained receipt is not yet part of this account. A native local run of the same harness was used only to validate operands while constructing it and is not offered as evidence.; Both prefix cases restart a single-voter cluster on an in-memory transport with an in-memory commit-tracking log store, so the observation window (1.5s = 30 election timeouts) measures non-participation of that node, not of a multi-node cluster.; The stale-membership consequence for a leader's commit index is argued from the code path that builds the commitment from configurations.latest; no scenario in this batch partitions a multi-node cluster to show a commit decided by a member set that the committed configuration does not contain.; The persisted commit index is staged only on log writes (raft.go:1392-1405, 1553-1561), so its value in the stored prefix is not necessarily the highest committed index; this does not affect the ordering argument, since any staged value above 0 exceeds the snapshot index in these cases.

Historical interpretation `afb62091645f43ef86ef604794d29bc8-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes (bootstrap, election through the normal path, client applies, shutdown) on top of their retained log, stable and snapshot stores, twice per prefix shape, with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off, and observed each restarted node for 1500ms (30 election/heartbeat timeouts at 50ms, polled every 5ms). Comparison 0 (flag on, no snapshot) is violated by witness event 2: the node ended in state Follower, leader_observed false over 268 polls, with latest_config_index 0, latest_config_ids empty and latest_config_voters 0, while the same prefix's log holds LogConfiguration@1 with one voter (event 0) and the persisted commit index was 5 (event 1). Comparison 1 (flag off, same prefix shape) holds at event 6: leader_observed true, latest_config_index 1 and latest_config_ids equal to the log's entry. Comparison 2 (flag on) is violated by witness event 3: recovered configuration index 0 with no servers against log_last_config_index 1 and log_last_config_ids carrying the single voter. Comparison 3 (flag off) holds at event 7 with config_matches_last_log_config true. Comparison 4 (flag on, snapshot at index 6 followed by a committed AddNonvoter at index 7) is violated by witness event 11: recovered latest_config_index 1 with exactly one voter and zero nonvoters (the snapshot's committed configuration, snapshot_index 6 in event 8), while the node's own log's newest configuration entry is index 7 with voters 1 and nonvoters 1 including the added peer. Comparison 5 (flag off, same prefix shape) holds at event 15 with the recovered configuration at index 7 including that peer. The controls differ from the violations only in the flag, on prefixes built the same way, so the difference tracks the replay path and not the prefix shape. Source side, the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs reads the persisted commit index, stores it and calls processLogs, which advances lastApplied to it (api.go:723-754), and only afterwards does NewRaft run the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state, starting at max(lastSnapshotIndex, lastApplied) + 1 (api.go:602-621). With the flag off lastApplied is the snapshot index and the scan covers the log; with the flag on it is the commit index, so committed configuration entries at or below it are never passed to processConfigurationLogEntry, the only writer of r.configurations (raft.go:1605-1624). The consequence recorded here is that the restarted node's decision state can be the snapshot's configuration or an empty one: with latest_config_index 0 the follower branch refuses to start an election (raft.go:225-241), which is terminal for the no-snapshot case because no reachable peer can ever send a configuration entry, and the stale view is what quorumSize, the leader's commitment membership and the vote-admission checks consume (raft.go:456-467, 1085-1094; commitment.go:35-64). Defect statement: with RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so a restarted node may keep an older or empty membership even though its log already contains the committed configuration entries; it can then be permanently unable to participate, or decide quorum from a member set that the committed configuration does not match. The duty is not documented as waived: the flag's doc comment speaks only about catching up the FSM (config.go:243-254) and says nothing about membership, while the startup scan exists precisely to rebuild that state from the log. The existing test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path.

Sources: [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json), [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_follower_election_guard](state.json), [src_commitment_from_config](state.json), [src_quorum_size](state.json), [src_try_stage_commit_index](state.json), [src_append_entries_staging](state.json), [src_existing_restore_test](state.json), [src_restore_committed_docs](state.json); execution evidence: [9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/check.json) ([observations](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/observations.json), event indices [2, 3, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15]).
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the replay merely moves its lower bound; nothing else reconstructs it afterwards.; In the snapshot case the node with the stale configuration nevertheless became leader and committed its no-op (event 10, commit_index 10), so this receipt does not show a wrong decision in that scenario: the omitted member was a nonvoter, so the majority of both the stale and the real configuration is the local node. This limits the demonstrated harm to losing a committed membership change from the node's decision state, and leaves a quorum-based violation to the open gap.; One could attribute the no-snapshot non-leadership to the observation window rather than the empty configuration. The control on the same prefix shape elected in 17 polls (about 85ms, event 6) while the feature-on node sat at 268 polls with latest_config_index 0 (event 2), and with no peers in that scenario no producer of AppendEntries or configuration entries exists, so the state cannot change on any later timescale.; Configuration entries below the persisted commit index were also delivered to the FSM as commit tuples during the replay, so an FSM implementing ConfigurationStore has observed them. That does not restore r.configurations, which is raft's own decision input and is never read back from the FSM.
Interpretation limitations: All four cases restart a single-voter cluster over an in-memory transport with an in-memory commit-tracking log store; the non-participation result is about that restarted node, not about cluster availability in a multi-node deployment.; The membership difference observed in the snapshot case involves a nonvoter, so no quorum arithmetic differed between the stale and the real configuration here; the quorum consequence is argued from the code paths that consume configurations.latest and remains the open gap.; No production log store is exercised; the probe relies on a store exposing a persisted commit index, and does not verify the caller-side atomicity contract of CommitTrackingLogStore.; One prefix per case; the receipt shows schedule-independent consistency across two independent prefix constructions per shape, but not repetition of the same prefix.; The persisted commit index is staged only on log writes (raft.go:1392-1405, 1553-1561), so the value read at restart need not be the highest committed index; in these cases it is 5 and 8 respectively, both above the relevant snapshot index, which is the only ordering the argument uses.
Gap responses: 98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-gap-0: model declares resolved: At startup only two inputs write the tracked configuration state, in this order: restoreSnapshot copies the snapshot's committed configuration and its index into both committed and latest (api.go:645-684), and the scan at api.go:610-621 replays processConfigurationLogEntry over log indices from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 upward. The restored FSM is not a source: the ConfigurationStore interface is write-only from raft's perspective (fsm.go:110-119 invokes StoreConfiguration; nothing reads it back), so the log scan is the only input that can roll the tracked configuration forward through the log. This is corroborated by the retained receipt: in the two feature-on cases the recovered configuration was exactly the snapshot's committed configuration (event 11: index 1, one voter, no nonvoters) or the empty configuration (events 2 and 3: index 0, no servers), never the newest configuration entry in the node's own log (index 1 in the no-snapshot case per event 0, index 7 in the snapshot case per event 8).; 98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-gap-1: gap remains open: Partially answered. The receipt shows one decision that the stale view changed outside the node: in the snapshot case with the flag on, the node reached leadership (event 10: final_state Leader, leader_observed true, commit_index and last_applied 10) while its tracked configuration was the snapshot's index-1 configuration with one voter and no nonvoters, not its log's committed index-7 configuration (event 11). That election was nevertheless still justifiable under the real committed configuration, because the omitted member was a nonvoter and the voting majority of both member sets is the local node, so the observed election is not yet a quorum formed from members the committed configuration excludes. A commit index advanced by a stale voter set, for example after a committed removal or a committed demotion, remains open and is not established by this receipt.

Historical interpretation `47aa9318d7fb43729a63a57146a1ea26-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off. With the flag on and no snapshot, the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: latest_config_index 0, no members, 268 polls with no leader) even though its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), while the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself in 17 polls (event 6); with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter, the flag-on node recovered the snapshot's index-1 configuration and never the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11), while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side, the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), which advances lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621); committed configuration entries at or below that bound are therefore never passed to processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624), and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is not read back. The tracked configuration is what decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and the vote-admission checks. Consequence, now observed. Receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a builds three voters A, B and C (event 0), snapshots A while the committed configuration is still {A,B,C} (event 1, snapshot index 4), removes C so the committed configuration becomes {A,B} at log index 5 (event 2), then restarts A with the flag on while B is partitioned and C stays connected. A's tracked configuration is the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration and it contains C (event 3: latest_config_index 1, latest_config_voters 3, latest_config_ids including C, log_config_index 5, log_config_voters 2). A then won the election - counting C's vote, a server the committed configuration no longer contains - and, in event 4, applied the client command 'stale-quorum-entry' with apply_error null at index 10 term 4, advancing its commit index and applied index to 10, while B's log does not contain that index and C's log contains it at the same term 4. A's own match index alone cannot form a majority of three, and B had no transport path to A, so the second match the commitment counted can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied to A's FSM and reported successful to the client by a quorum that is not a majority of the committed configuration {A,B}. Defect account for the whole question: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so a restarted node may keep an older or empty membership although its log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can then be permanently unable to participate (empty configuration), or, as observed here, decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match, committing an entry that a real voter of the committed configuration does not hold. The failed predecessor execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution of this interpretation: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step with 'nothing new to snapshot' because the elected leader was not A and A had applied no entry, so it produced no comparison result about the target. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path.

Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the replay merely moves its lower bound; the observed consequence is a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain.; The violating scenario needs the removed server C to stay running and reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted. If an operator removes a server and also stops it, or if it is unreachable, the same stale configuration would instead leave A unable to commit, so the receipt shows the reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; The harness chose A as the restarting leader and B as the partitioned node, and it moved leadership to A with the library's leadership transfer before the snapshot; those are setup choices. They do not supply the stale configuration (event 3 shows A tracking a configuration its log had already superseded) and they do not supply the acknowledgement (C's log holds the entry at the same term), but a different assignment of roles is not exercised.; The receipt shows that the leader committed and applied the entry and returned success to the caller, and that the committed configuration's other voter lacked it; it does not follow the entry to a later term to observe an actual loss from a state machine, so the demonstrated violation is of the commitment rule rather than a completed divergence.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1, 'snapshot on A failed: nothing new to snapshot'), and its single parsed event carries no configuration operands; it is retained as a harness artifact and not as evidence about the target.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process, with a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one restart and one committed removal; the empty-configuration case is single-voter only, so the multi-node consequence of an empty recovered configuration (a voter that can never participate) is argued rather than observed.; The removed server is kept running deliberately, and no message loss, reordering or crash of the leader is injected, so the observation is a decision defect under a clean transport rather than a fault-injection study.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node harness was run four times during construction with the same witness, and the retained receipt is a single execution.
Gap responses: 98ee8dfd5fac4b84915f91ed4b094bcf-gap-1: model declares resolved: Resolved by receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a. The stale recovered configuration changed two decisions observable outside the node: the election A won (event 4, a_became_leader true) counted C, a server the committed configuration had removed, towards the majority of its tracked three-voter configuration, and the commit that followed (event 4: entry index 10, term 4, apply_error null, a_commit_index 10, a_applied_index 10, a_fsm_applied_payload true) was decided while the committed configuration's other voter B, partitioned from A, did not hold the entry (b_log_has_entry false) and the removed server C did (c_log_has_entry true, c_entry_term 4). Because A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path to A, the second match must have come from C, so the commit rule was applied against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration {A,B}.

Historical interpretation `9e3f8a75105f44bdab6c207c54235de8-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

Sources: [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json), [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_follower_election_guard](state.json), [src_setup_leader_state](state.json), [src_commitment_from_config](state.json), [src_quorum_size](state.json), [src_follower_append_entries](state.json), [src_snapshot_carries_config](state.json), [src_restore_snapshot_config](state.json), [src_restore_committed_docs](state.json); execution evidence: [9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/check.json) ([observations](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/observations.json), event indices [2, 3, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15]); [79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/check.json) ([observations](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]); [c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/check.json) ([observations](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]).
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the observed consequences are a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain and a durable record that re-imposes the loss.; The commit-rule violation needs the removed server C to stay reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted; if it were stopped or unreachable the same stale configuration would instead leave the node unable to commit. The receipt therefore shows one reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; One could argue the snapshot in the durable-state case is only as good as the state the node tracks, and that a caller should not snapshot a node whose configuration is empty. The counter is that the node is an ordinary cluster member whose own log contains the configuration entries, nothing in the API or the documentation marks its snapshots as unusable, and the resulting metadata then defeats a restart that has the feature disabled, so the corruption outlives the condition that created it.; The flag-off restart's failure to lead could be attributed to the 1500ms observation window rather than to the empty configuration. The counter is the source guard that refuses elections while configurations.latestIndex is zero (raft.go:225-241), together with the sibling controls on the same machinery that elected within about 85ms (receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c, event 6); 1500ms is 30 election timeouts.; The harness assigns roles deliberately (A restarts, B is partitioned, leadership is moved with the library's transfer) and it takes the snapshot explicitly rather than through the automatic cadence. Those choices select the scenario but do not supply the stale or empty configuration, which event 6 shows against the node's own log, nor the acknowledgement, which C's log carries.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469) and is retained as a harness artifact; the later receipts contain the same scenario with both defects exercised.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process and the harness uses a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified, and no production store or snapshot store is exercised.; The durable-state case snapshots a node that has no snapshot on its stores and then restarts with the replay disabled; shipping such a snapshot to a follower, or corruption arising from the automatic snapshot cadence, is not exercised, and deleting the corrupt snapshot is not tested as a recovery.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one committed removal, keeps the removed server running, and injects no message loss, reordering or crash, so the decision defect is observed under a clean transport.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node scenario appears in two receipts with the same operands, the durable-state scenario in one, and the observation windows are 1500ms for non-participation and 2000ms apply timeouts.

Historical interpretation `87e05159dda24b4fbd32cabaea9f4304-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only by a transcribed latency; the control's election is now stated as the receipt records it (19 polls at a 5ms interval, about 95ms), not from an earlier construction run. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence and limitations - is unchanged. Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

Historical interpretation `8dd0e551241244af988706dc6380ba58-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Extension note: this successor reports the repeat of the multi-node experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1), which reproduced the commit-rule, durable-snapshot and availability cases with identical operands but not the authority witness, because the caller's persisted commit index was zero in that execution and the startup scan therefore recovered the configuration; the authority paragraph now states that precondition and the counterevidence records it. Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration.
