Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). The precondition is visible in the same operand: the empty configuration appears when the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, because that index becomes lastApplied and pushes the scan above the log's configuration entry. The repeat of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

Sources: [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json), [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_follower_election_guard](state.json), [src_setup_leader_state](state.json), [src_commitment_from_config](state.json), [src_quorum_size](state.json), [src_follower_append_entries](state.json), [src_snapshot_carries_config](state.json), [src_restore_snapshot_config](state.json), [src_restore_committed_docs](state.json); execution evidence: [9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/check.json) ([observations](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/observations.json), event indices [2, 3, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15]); [79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/check.json) ([observations](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]); [c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/check.json) ([observations](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]); [4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332](logs/4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332/check.json) ([observations](logs/4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332/observations.json), event indices [15, 16, 17, 18]); [fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1](logs/fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1/check.json) ([observations](logs/fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1/observations.json), event indices [4, 6, 7, 8, 11, 13, 15, 16, 17, 18]).
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the observed consequences are a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain and a durable record that re-imposes the loss.; The commit-rule violation needs the removed server C to stay reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted; if it were stopped or unreachable the same stale configuration would instead leave the node unable to commit. The receipt therefore shows one reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; One could argue the snapshot in the durable-state case is only as good as the state the node tracks, and that a caller should not snapshot a node whose configuration is empty. The counter is that the node is an ordinary cluster member whose own log contains the configuration entries, nothing in the API or the documentation marks its snapshots as unusable, and the resulting metadata then defeats a restart that has the feature disabled, so the corruption outlives the condition that created it.; The flag-off restart's failure to lead could be attributed to the 1500ms observation window rather than to the empty configuration. The counter is the source guard that refuses elections while configurations.latestIndex is zero (raft.go:225-241) plus two controls inside the same window: the single-voter control elected itself in 19 polls of the harness's 5ms interval (receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c, event 6), and in the authority case the node that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples while the node with the empty configuration showed zero candidate samples (receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, events 16 and 18).; The harness assigns roles deliberately (A restarts, B is partitioned, leadership is moved with the library's transfer) and it takes the snapshot explicitly rather than through the automatic cadence. Those choices select the scenario but do not supply the stale or empty configuration, which event 6 shows against the node's own log, nor the acknowledgement, which C's log carries.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469) and is retained as a harness artifact; the later receipts contain the same scenario with both defects exercised.; The authority case removes the other voters by shutting them down, so it shows that the defective node never attempts candidacy; it does not show an outage that a correct recovery would have prevented, because the recovered three-voter configuration also lacks a quorum with one voter left. What the receipt adds is the capability difference, not a counterfactual outage.; The authority witness depends on a property of the caller's store: A's persisted commit index must be non-zero, because that value becomes lastApplied and pushes the startup scan above the log's configuration entry. The repeat shows the other side of that condition - with a persisted commit index of 0 the scan starts at index 1, A recovers its configuration and does attempt candidacy (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1, events 15 and 16). The claim is therefore bounded to restarts whose store reports a non-zero commit index, which the original receipt's operand (staged commit index 4) shows for its own execution.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process and the harness uses a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified, and no production store or snapshot store is exercised.; The durable-state case snapshots a node that has no snapshot on its stores and then restarts with the replay disabled; shipping such a snapshot to a follower, or corruption arising from the automatic snapshot cadence, is not exercised, and deleting the corrupt snapshot is not tested as a recovery.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one committed removal, keeps the removed server running, and injects no message loss, reordering or crash, so the decision defect is observed under a clean transport.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node scenario appears in two receipts with the same operands, the durable-state scenario in one, and the observation windows are 1500ms for non-participation and 2000ms apply timeouts.; The authority witness has one execution in which its premise held and one repeat in which it did not, so it is currently a single valid witness; a revised experiment that keeps committing until A's persisted commit index is non-zero was submitted with this batch and its receipt is not yet part of this account.

Historical interpretation `64f67925189748089ddaff8f0edb7547-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Extension note: this successor cites the revised authority experiment (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357), whose harness keeps committing until the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index before restarting the node; the witness reappeared under that explicit premise and the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor reports the repeat of the multi-node experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1), which reproduced the commit-rule, durable-snapshot and availability cases with identical operands but not the authority witness, because the caller's persisted commit index was zero in that execution and the startup scan therefore recovered the configuration; the authority paragraph now states that precondition and the counterevidence records it. A revised experiment that holds the precondition explicitly is submitted with this batch. Extension note: this successor adds receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, which observes in a three-voter group that a node recovering without a configuration never attempts candidacy while the same node and log with the replay disabled do; the sources, the three earlier executions, the knowledge uses, the conclusion and the remaining counterevidence are otherwise unchanged, and the counter about the observation window is strengthened with the new control's sample counts. Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only by a transcribed latency; the control's election is now stated as the receipt records it (19 polls at a 5ms interval, about 95ms), not from an earlier construction run. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence and limitations - is unchanged. Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Authority attempt. Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). A node whose tracked configuration is empty therefore does not merely fail to win an election: it never attempts to acquire authority, so it cannot be the source of a new leader even when the surviving members could form a quorum with it. The precondition is visible in the same operand: the empty configuration appears when the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, because that index becomes lastApplied and pushes the scan above the log's configuration entry. The revised experiment holds the premise explicitly and the witness reappeared: keeping the leader committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, A again tracked no configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4, with no extra applies needed) and again never entered Candidate in the sampling window while the control that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 257 samples (events 16 and 18, receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, comparisons violated and holds). That receipt also ran the other three cases a further time with the same operands (events 4, 6 to 8, 11 and 13). The repeat of the earlier version of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

The revised experiment holds the premise explicitly and the witness reappeared: keeping the leader committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, A again tracked no configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4, with no extra applies needed) and again never entered Candidate in the sampling window while the control that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 257 samples (events 16 and 18, receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, comparisons violated and holds). That receipt also ran the other three cases a further time with the same operands (events 4, 6 to 8, 11 and 13). The repeat of the earlier version of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target.

Sources: [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json), [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_follower_election_guard](state.json), [src_setup_leader_state](state.json), [src_commitment_from_config](state.json), [src_quorum_size](state.json), [src_follower_append_entries](state.json), [src_snapshot_carries_config](state.json), [src_restore_snapshot_config](state.json), [src_restore_committed_docs](state.json); execution evidence: [9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/check.json) ([observations](logs/9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c/observations.json), event indices [2, 3, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15]); [79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/check.json) ([observations](logs/79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]); [c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/check.json) ([observations](logs/c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5/observations.json), event indices [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]); [4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332](logs/4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332/check.json) ([observations](logs/4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332/observations.json), event indices [15, 16, 17, 18]); [fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1](logs/fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1/check.json) ([observations](logs/fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1/observations.json), event indices [4, 6, 7, 8, 11, 13, 15, 16, 17, 18]); [67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357](logs/67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357/check.json) ([observations](logs/67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357/observations.json), event indices [4, 6, 7, 8, 11, 13, 15, 16, 17, 18]); [b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1](logs/b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1/check.json) ([observations](logs/b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1/observations.json), event indices [19, 20]).
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the observed consequences are a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain and a durable record that re-imposes the loss.; The commit-rule violation needs the removed server C to stay reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted; if it were stopped or unreachable the same stale configuration would instead leave the node unable to commit. The receipt therefore shows one reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; One could argue the snapshot in the durable-state case is only as good as the state the node tracks, and that a caller should not snapshot a node whose configuration is empty. The counter is that the node is an ordinary cluster member whose own log contains the configuration entries, nothing in the API or the documentation marks its snapshots as unusable, and the resulting metadata then defeats a restart that has the feature disabled, so the corruption outlives the condition that created it.; The flag-off restart's failure to lead could be attributed to the 1500ms observation window rather than to the empty configuration. The counter is the source guard that refuses elections while configurations.latestIndex is zero (raft.go:225-241) plus two controls inside the same window: the single-voter control elected itself in 19 polls of the harness's 5ms interval (receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c, event 6), and in the authority case the node that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples while the node with the empty configuration showed zero candidate samples (receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, events 16 and 18).; The harness assigns roles deliberately (A restarts, B is partitioned, leadership is moved with the library's transfer) and it takes the snapshot explicitly rather than through the automatic cadence. Those choices select the scenario but do not supply the stale or empty configuration, which event 6 shows against the node's own log, nor the acknowledgement, which C's log carries.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469) and is retained as a harness artifact; the later receipts contain the same scenario with both defects exercised.; The authority case removes the other voters by shutting them down, so it shows that the defective node never attempts candidacy; it does not show an outage that a correct recovery would have prevented, because the recovered three-voter configuration also lacks a quorum with one voter left. What the receipt adds is the capability difference, not a counterfactual outage.; The authority witness depends on a property of the caller's store: A's persisted commit index must be non-zero, because that value becomes lastApplied and pushes the startup scan above the log's configuration entry. The repeat shows the other side of that condition - with a persisted commit index of 0 the scan starts at index 1, A recovers its configuration and does attempt candidacy (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1, events 15 and 16). The claim is therefore bounded to restarts whose store reports a non-zero commit index: the revised experiment establishes that premise explicitly and the witness reappears (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, staged commit index 4 with no extra applies needed, zero candidate samples), while the repeat that lacked the premise shows the other side. Both valid executions therefore share the same precondition, which their staged_commit_index operands make inspectable.; The authority and configuration claims must not be read as the node leaving the cluster: in a two-voter group the recovered node with an empty tracked configuration still voted, so its peer was elected with a majority of two (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1, events 19 and 20). The node's limitation is candidacy rather than participation, and any consequence that assumes it is absent from every future majority would overstate the effect.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process and the harness uses a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified, and no production store or snapshot store is exercised.; The durable-state case snapshots a node that has no snapshot on its stores and then restarts with the replay disabled; shipping such a snapshot to a follower, or corruption arising from the automatic snapshot cadence, is not exercised, and deleting the corrupt snapshot is not tested as a recovery.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one committed removal, keeps the removed server running, and injects no message loss, reordering or crash, so the decision defect is observed under a clean transport.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node scenario appears in two receipts with the same operands, the durable-state scenario in one, and the observation windows are 1500ms for non-participation and 2000ms apply timeouts.; The authority witness now has two valid executions and one repeat in which its premise did not hold; both valid executions report the same precondition operand (a staged commit index of 4) and the second was produced by a harness that enforces it, so the remaining gap is only that the precondition itself comes from the caller's store behaviour rather than from anything the test controls end to end.

Historical interpretation `8d521d6ed34941308ba2f976b1f41dc6-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Extension note: this successor cites the repeat of the two-voter probe (receipt 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195), which returns the same split as the first execution, so the boundary that a node recovering without a configuration still votes rests on two runs; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the two-voter probe (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1), which shows that a node recovering without a configuration still grants a vote to a healthy peer while never becoming a candidate itself; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the revised authority experiment (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357), whose harness keeps committing until the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index before restarting the node; the witness reappeared under that explicit premise and the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor reports the repeat of the multi-node experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1), which reproduced the commit-rule, durable-snapshot and availability cases with identical operands but not the authority witness, because the caller's persisted commit index was zero in that execution and the startup scan therefore recovered the configuration; the authority paragraph now states that precondition and the counterevidence records it. A revised experiment that holds the precondition explicitly is submitted with this batch. Extension note: this successor adds receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, which observes in a three-voter group that a node recovering without a configuration never attempts candidacy while the same node and log with the replay disabled do; the sources, the three earlier executions, the knowledge uses, the conclusion and the remaining counterevidence are otherwise unchanged, and the counter about the observation window is strengthened with the new control's sample counts. Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only by a transcribed latency; the control's election is now stated as the receipt records it (19 polls at a 5ms interval, about 95ms), not from an earlier construction run. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence and limitations - is unchanged. Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Authority attempt. Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). A node whose tracked configuration is empty therefore does not merely fail to win an election: it never attempts to acquire authority, so it cannot be the source of a new leader even when the surviving members could form a quorum with it. The claim is bounded on the other side by a two-voter probe: receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 shows the same recovered node tracking no configuration (event 19: latest_config_index 0, no members, with the group size recorded as two) and, when its peer is restarted so that it must campaign, the peer is elected - because a majority of two is both members, that leadership required the defective node's vote - while the defective node again showed zero candidate samples in the sampling window (event 20, comparison holds). The defect therefore removes the node as a candidate, not as a voter: it can still support a peer's candidacy, and its harm is that it cannot itself be the source of a new leader. The precondition is visible in the same operand: the empty configuration appears when the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, because that index becomes lastApplied and pushes the scan above the log's configuration entry. The revised experiment holds the premise explicitly and the witness reappeared: keeping the leader committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, A again tracked no configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4, with no extra applies needed) and again never entered Candidate in the sampling window while the control that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 257 samples (events 16 and 18, receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, comparisons violated and holds). That receipt also ran the other three cases a further time with the same operands (events 4, 6 to 8, 11 and 13). The repeat of the earlier version of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target. The two-voter probe has now been executed twice from the same retained inputs: in both runs the recovered node tracks no configuration (staged commit index 5, latest_config_index 0, no members), shows zero candidate samples while its peer campaigns, and the peer reaches Leader in a group of two, which requires that node's vote (receipts b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 and 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195, events 19 and 20 in each).

Historical interpretation `54dee3b26c0e415f90b11dd070752bc2-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold; model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification): Correction note: an earlier statement in this account described processConfigurationLogEntry as the only writer of the tracked configuration state, which is too strong - restoreSnapshot installs the newest local snapshot's committed configuration (api.go:677-678), installSnapshot installs the metadata of a streamed snapshot (raft.go:1960-1961), appendConfigurationEntry records the configuration it appends on the leader (raft.go:1238), and the conflict path in appendEntries rolls the latest configuration back to the committed one (raft.go:1548) - while processConfigurationLogEntry remains the writer for entries taken from the log, called from appendEntries and from the startup scan. The mechanism the account relies on is unchanged and in fact sharpened: the startup inputs are the snapshot's configuration and the log scan, and the scan's lower bound is what the feature moves. Extension note: this successor cites the repeat of the two-voter probe (receipt 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195), which returns the same split as the first execution, so the boundary that a node recovering without a configuration still votes rests on two runs; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the two-voter probe (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1), which shows that a node recovering without a configuration still grants a vote to a healthy peer while never becoming a candidate itself; the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the revised authority experiment (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357), whose harness keeps committing until the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index before restarting the node; the witness reappeared under that explicit premise and the account's other parts are unchanged. Extension note: this successor reports the repeat of the multi-node experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1), which reproduced the commit-rule, durable-snapshot and availability cases with identical operands but not the authority witness, because the caller's persisted commit index was zero in that execution and the startup scan therefore recovered the configuration; the authority paragraph now states that precondition and the counterevidence records it. A revised experiment that holds the precondition explicitly is submitted with this batch. Extension note: this successor adds receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, which observes in a three-voter group that a node recovering without a configuration never attempts candidacy while the same node and log with the replay disabled do; the sources, the three earlier executions, the knowledge uses, the conclusion and the remaining counterevidence are otherwise unchanged, and the counter about the observation window is strengthened with the new control's sample counts. Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only by a transcribed latency; the control's election is now stated as the receipt records it (19 polls at a 5ms interval, about 95ms), not from an earlier construction run. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence and limitations - is unchanged. Recovery. Receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c restarted four real single-voter prefixes on their retained stores with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs on and off: with the flag on and no snapshot the node recovered an empty configuration (event 2: no members at index 0, 268 polls without a leader) although its log holds the bootstrap configuration entry at index 1 (event 0), the flag-off control on the same prefix shape tracked that entry and elected itself (event 6), and with a snapshot taken before a committed AddNonvoter the flag-on node kept the snapshot's index-1 configuration instead of the log's committed index-7 configuration (events 10, 11) while the flag-off control recovered index 7 (event 15). Source side the mechanism is settled: restoreFromCommittedLogs stores the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it (api.go:723-754), advancing lastApplied before NewRaft runs the scan that rebuilds tracked configuration state from max(snapshotIndex, lastApplied)+1 (api.go:602-621), so committed configuration entries at or below that bound never reach processConfigurationLogEntry (raft.go:1605-1624) and the FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back. That state decides election eligibility (raft.go:225-246), quorum size (raft.go:1085-1094), the leader's commitment membership (raft.go:456-467, commitment.go:35-64) and vote admission. First consequence: receipt 79a9cc34b3ce42e197c384775efecc3a removed server C from {A,B,C} (event 2: committed configuration at index 5 with two voters), restarted A with the flag on while B was partitioned, and A tracked the snapshot's index-1 three-voter configuration containing C (event 3); A won the election counting C and committed 'stale-quorum-entry' at index 10 term 4 with apply_error null, its FSM applied it, B's log lacked that index and C's log held it at the same term (event 4). Since A's own match cannot form a majority of three and B had no transport path, the deciding acknowledgement can only have come from C: the entry was committed, applied and reported successful against a member set that is not a majority of the committed configuration. Authority attempt. Receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332 builds a three-voter group, commits three commands so the staged commit index is non-zero, restarts A over its own stores and reads its tracked configuration: with the replay enabled A tracks no configuration at all (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4), and in 266 samples over 1500ms of hearing no leader with the other voters shut down it was a follower in every sample and never a candidate (event 16, comparison violated); with the replay disabled the same node and the same log recover the three-voter configuration (event 17: latest_config_index 1, three members) and the node was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples, ending in state Candidate (event 18, comparison holds). A node whose tracked configuration is empty therefore does not merely fail to win an election: it never attempts to acquire authority, so it cannot be the source of a new leader even when the surviving members could form a quorum with it. The claim is bounded on the other side by a two-voter probe: receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 shows the same recovered node tracking no configuration (event 19: latest_config_index 0, no members, with the group size recorded as two) and, when its peer is restarted so that it must campaign, the peer is elected - because a majority of two is both members, that leadership required the defective node's vote - while the defective node again showed zero candidate samples in the sampling window (event 20, comparison holds). The defect therefore removes the node as a candidate, not as a voter: it can still support a peer's candidacy, and its harm is that it cannot itself be the source of a new leader. The precondition is visible in the same operand: the empty configuration appears when the caller's store reports a non-zero persisted commit index, because that index becomes lastApplied and pushes the scan above the log's configuration entry. The revised experiment holds the premise explicitly and the witness reappeared: keeping the leader committing until A's store reports a non-zero commit index, A again tracked no configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 0, no members, staged commit index 4, with no extra applies needed) and again never entered Candidate in the sampling window while the control that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 257 samples (events 16 and 18, receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, comparisons violated and holds). That receipt also ran the other three cases a further time with the same operands (events 4, 6 to 8, 11 and 13). The repeat of the earlier version of this experiment (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1) makes the dependence explicit: there A's persisted commit index was 0, so restoreFromCommittedLogs left lastApplied at zero, the scan started at index 1, A recovered the three-voter configuration (event 15: latest_config_index 1 with three members although the replay was enabled) and it did attempt candidacy, 255 candidate samples in 267 (event 16), which is why that execution's comparison returned unknown rather than a witness. The same repeat reproduced the other three cases with identical operands: the commit-rule case again tracked the snapshot's three-voter configuration against a log whose configuration entry has two voters and again committed with only the removed server's acknowledgement (events 2, 3, 4), the durable-snapshot case again wrote an empty configuration at index 0 into the snapshot metadata and again came up empty after a flag-off restart (events 6, 7, 8), and the availability case again committed with two members live and lost the ability after the second stop (events 11, 13). Second consequence, durable: receipt c477da09810f4842ba55130320e2a6b5 leaves A in that recovered state on a three-voter cluster (event 6: latest_config_index 0 with no members and committed_config_index 0, against its log's index-1 three-voter entry and staged commit index 4), takes a snapshot through the library's normal path, and reads back metadata recording no configuration at index 0 while the log establishes a three-voter configuration at index 1 (event 7, snapshot_config_matches_log_config false). A then restarts on the same stores with the replay disabled and comes up with an empty configuration at index 0, no leader within 1500ms, in state Follower (event 8). The loss is therefore not confined to the replay path: once written, the snapshot metadata re-imposes it on a restart that never enables the feature. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled and a commit-tracking log store, NewRaft cannot roll the node's tracked membership configuration forward past max(snapshot index, persisted commit index), so the node may keep an older or empty membership although its own log already contains the committed configuration entries; the recovered node can be permanently unable to participate, can decide leadership and commit index from a member set the committed configuration does not match - committing an entry a real voter of that configuration does not hold - and can write the lost configuration into durable snapshot metadata, from which later restarts, including ones with the feature disabled, restore it. The library's own test for the feature (raft_test.go:1183-1270) restarts with a snapshot whose configuration already equals the final configuration and asserts only the applied index, so it does not constrain this path. The failed first execution of the multi-node experiment (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469, exit 1) is retained in the run's logs but is not cited as an execution here: it stopped in the harness at the snapshot step because the elected leader was not the node being snapshotted, so it produced no comparison result about the target. The two-voter probe has now been executed twice from the same retained inputs: in both runs the recovered node tracks no configuration (staged commit index 5, latest_config_index 0, no members), shows zero candidate samples while its peer campaigns, and the peer reaches Leader in a group of two, which requires that node's vote (receipts b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1 and 0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195, events 19 and 20 in each).

[b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1](logs/b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1/check.json) ([observations](logs/b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1/observations.json), event indices [19, 20]); [0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195](logs/0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195/check.json) ([observations](logs/0abdaee65d5d40159726504b73daa195/observations.json), event indices [19, 20]).
Interpretation counterevidence: The doc comment for RestoreCommittedLogs (config.go:243-254) promises only that committed logs are replayed to catch the FSM up and says nothing about membership recovery, so the stale tracked configuration can be read as an unnamed side effect of a feature that never claimed to preserve it. The counter is that the startup scan at api.go:610-621 exists to rebuild that state from the log, and the observed consequences are a commit decided against a member set the committed configuration does not contain and a durable record that re-imposes the loss.; The commit-rule violation needs the removed server C to stay reachable after its removal, since its acknowledgement is what the stale majority counted; if it were stopped or unreachable the same stale configuration would instead leave the node unable to commit. The receipt therefore shows one reachable-removed-server case rather than every case.; One could argue the snapshot in the durable-state case is only as good as the state the node tracks, and that a caller should not snapshot a node whose configuration is empty. The counter is that the node is an ordinary cluster member whose own log contains the configuration entries, nothing in the API or the documentation marks its snapshots as unusable, and the resulting metadata then defeats a restart that has the feature disabled, so the corruption outlives the condition that created it.; The flag-off restart's failure to lead could be attributed to the 1500ms observation window rather than to the empty configuration. The counter is the source guard that refuses elections while configurations.latestIndex is zero (raft.go:225-241) plus two controls inside the same window: the single-voter control elected itself in 19 polls of the harness's 5ms interval (receipt 9a373c13725a42aa9f48f59c0f2f2f1c, event 6), and in the authority case the node that recovered its configuration was a candidate in 254 of 266 samples while the node with the empty configuration showed zero candidate samples (receipt 4178d9d5c479470cafb7ebd7951a9332, events 16 and 18).; The harness assigns roles deliberately (A restarts, B is partitioned, leadership is moved with the library's transfer) and it takes the snapshot explicitly rather than through the automatic cadence. Those choices select the scenario but do not supply the stale or empty configuration, which event 6 shows against the node's own log, nor the acknowledgement, which C's log carries.; The first execution of the multi-node experiment failed in the harness before any comparison (receipt 4ed46b198c8d41618cc855df0a63d469) and is retained as a harness artifact; the later receipts contain the same scenario with both defects exercised.; The authority case removes the other voters by shutting them down, so it shows that the defective node never attempts candidacy; it does not show an outage that a correct recovery would have prevented, because the recovered three-voter configuration also lacks a quorum with one voter left. What the receipt adds is the capability difference, not a counterfactual outage.; The authority witness depends on a property of the caller's store: A's persisted commit index must be non-zero, because that value becomes lastApplied and pushes the startup scan above the log's configuration entry. The repeat shows the other side of that condition - with a persisted commit index of 0 the scan starts at index 1, A recovers its configuration and does attempt candidacy (receipt fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1, events 15 and 16). The claim is therefore bounded to restarts whose store reports a non-zero commit index: the revised experiment establishes that premise explicitly and the witness reappears (receipt 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, staged commit index 4 with no extra applies needed, zero candidate samples), while the repeat that lacked the premise shows the other side. Both valid executions therefore share the same precondition, which their staged_commit_index operands make inspectable.; The authority and configuration claims must not be read as the node leaving the cluster: in a two-voter group the recovered node with an empty tracked configuration still voted, so its peer was elected with a majority of two (receipt b999288258be402cb987482ced5256c1, events 19 and 20). The node's limitation is candidacy rather than participation, and any consequence that assumes it is absent from every future majority would overstate the effect.; The two-voter vote result is reproduced rather than sampled once, but both runs use the same harness, the same two-voter shape and the same 3-second window; a peer whose log were behind the recovered node's would exercise a different branch of the vote checks, which is not covered.
Interpretation limitations: All nodes are in-memory transports and stores in one process and the harness uses a test log store whose staging is not atomic with its log writes; the caller-side durability contract of CommitTrackingLogStore is assumed, not verified, and no production store or snapshot store is exercised.; The durable-state case snapshots a node that has no snapshot on its stores and then restarts with the replay disabled; shipping such a snapshot to a follower, or corruption arising from the automatic snapshot cadence, is not exercised, and deleting the corrupt snapshot is not tested as a recovery.; The multi-node case is one prefix shape with one committed removal, keeps the removed server running, and injects no message loss, reordering or crash, so the decision defect is observed under a clean transport.; Schedule variation is limited: the multi-node scenario appears in two receipts with the same operands, the durable-state scenario in one, and the observation windows are 1500ms for non-participation and 2000ms apply timeouts.; The authority witness now has two valid executions and one repeat in which its premise did not hold; both valid executions report the same precondition operand (a staged commit index of 4) and the second was produced by a harness that enforces it, so the remaining gap is only that the precondition itself comes from the caller's store behaviour rather than from anything the test controls end to end.

Historical interpretation `8afcaa2327d94db1a6720eae0efbd4e3-interpretation-0` · source explanation; model defect claim: no explicit defect claim submitted: Correction account for the enumerated writers of raft's tracked configuration. Source: processConfigurationLogEntry sets the tracked state for LogConfiguration and deprecated peer entries taken from the log and is called by appendEntries for newly appended entries and by the NewRaft startup scan (raft.go:1595-1620, api.go:610-621); appendConfigurationEntry records the configuration the leader appends (raft.go:1238); the conflict path in appendEntries rolls latest back to committed when it deletes a suffix below the latest configuration index (raft.go:1548); restoreSnapshot installs the newest local snapshot's committed configuration and index at startup (api.go:677-678); and installSnapshot installs the metadata of a snapshot received from a leader (raft.go:1960-1961). The FSM's ConfigurationStore is never read back, so an FSM-side copy of the configuration is not a source of the node's decision state. The correction does not weaken any recorded conclusion, and it sharpens the account that prompted it: those conclusions turn on the startup inputs being the snapshot's configuration and the log scan, and on the scan's lower bound, which is exactly what the feature moves; the receipts show the resulting states directly (an empty configuration with a non-zero persisted commit index, the snapshot's older configuration with a zero one, and the recovered configuration when the scan runs).

Sources: [src_process_config_entry](state.json), [src_restore_snapshot_config](state.json), [src_newraft_startup](state.json), [src_restore_committed](state.json); execution evidence: No execution receipt explicitly cited by this interpretation.
Interpretation counterevidence: The correction is source-only: no receipt in this run exercises the conflict rollback or the streamed install snapshot path, so those writers are stated from the source rather than observed.; This account makes no defect judgment; the experiment-cited account for the question remains the defect account.
Interpretation limitations: The enumeration is taken from the call sites of the two setters; a writer reached through an interface or a code path not present in this snapshot would not appear in it.
Gap responses: 54dee3b26c0e415f90b11dd070752bc2-gap-9: model declares resolved: The corrected statement enumerates the writers: processConfigurationLogEntry for entries taken from the log, appendConfigurationEntry on the leader, the conflict rollback in appendEntries, restoreSnapshot for the local snapshot, and installSnapshot for a streamed one (raft.go:1238, 1548, 1595-1620, 1960-1961; api.go:677-678), with the FSM's ConfigurationStore never read back. Knowledge revision 2 states this, and the interpretation for this question cites it.
### Can the startup replay that Config.RestoreCommittedLogs performs fail to complete? restoreFromCommittedLogs applies committed entries through processLogs before NewRaft starts the FSM goroutine, and processLogs hands each batch to a fixed-capacity channel, so the completion of NewRaft may depend on how much committed log lies above the snapshot.

Current disposition: active.

Current model defect claim: defect asserted (subject to independent verification).

Current interpretation `336003f12bb242e8b65347b51645bdca-interpretation-0` · declared predicate does not hold: Consistency note: this successor merges two limitation entries that described the same boundary result differently - the older wording still said the boundary case used one batch size after the extended harness had measured it at two - so the account no longer contains contradictory statements about that setting; sources, executions, knowledge uses, conclusion and counterevidence are unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the extended boundary harness (receipt f394d8354fab48138079db19ca3deb13), which measures the boundary at the library's default batch size as well as at 16, so the account's central quantitative claim rests on two batch sizes; sources, the other executions, knowledge uses, conclusion and counterevidence are otherwise unchanged. Extension note: this successor cites the revised availability harness (receipt 7ac7e4f67d194ad5b4e196c9b73f9897), which adds a case at the default batch size and reproduces the consequence there, so the account's availability claim no longer rests on a single batch size; the other executions, sources, knowledge uses, conclusion and counterevidence are unchanged. Correction note: the behaviour this account relied on for the default snapshot cadence has been refined. The earlier statement described the automatic cadence as keeping the committed range above the snapshot near SnapshotThreshold; the default-configuration receipt shows a node whose committed range stood 12001 entries above an absent snapshot while the threshold was 8192 and no snapshot had been taken (receipt 0cb30b6bf8874d479b83653bd4e6c553, event 36), which is a margin of roughly one and a half times the threshold rather than a value near it. The cause is that runSnapshots evaluates shouldSnapshot only when a randomized timeout of up to SnapshotInterval expires (snapshot.go:71-95), so between checks the range can pass the threshold and keep growing. Knowledge revision 2 states this; the counterevidence about the burst workload and the statement that how long a node typically stays above the bound is unmeasured both still hold. Extension note: this successor cites the repeat of the bound experiment (receipt 962196ed81fd46cfb9476bbd63c2c637), which reproduced the below-bound and above-bound cases with identical operands, so every pair in this account now has at least two executions; sources, the other executions, knowledge uses, conclusion and counterevidence are otherwise unchanged. Extension note: this successor records that the availability pair has been executed three times, not once - the two later receipts were produced by the other investigation's multi-node harness, which contains this pair among its four cases - and corrects the schedule-variation limitation accordingly; sources, the other executions, knowledge uses, conclusion and counterevidence are unchanged. Extension note: this successor adds the second execution of the fixed experiment (receipt 9039d268ffc24fa085efc75de7dc4fb2) as reproducibility evidence for the boundary and default-configuration pairs; sources, knowledge uses, conclusion and the other executions are unchanged, and the counterevidence and limitation that described those pairs as single-run are updated accordingly. Precision note: the entry-based batch figures recorded by the earlier receipts equal the delivered counts there, and the receipts' own operands show why. In receipt 3d04552f58e548b0ae089192392b564f event 16 the prefix ends at log index 9002 with staged commit index 9001, so the replayed range holds the bootstrap configuration entry, one no-op and 8999 commands, that is 9000 tuples, and ceil(9000/64) = 141, the figure the receipt records for that case. Receipt e82594cc274340f0a961608cda519e00 records 1501 and 3001 replayed entries with 94 and 188 batches at MaxAppendEntries 16, and the same counting gives 1500 and 3000 tuples, ceil(1500/16) = 94 and ceil(3000/16) = 188. The two counts diverge only where the replayed range ends inside a batch in a way that adds a no-op, which is what the boundary receipt's 2049-entry range with 128 delivered batches shows. Reproducibility: the fixed experiment that produced the boundary and default-configuration pairs was executed a second time from its retained inputs in a clean copy (receipt 9039d268ffc24fa085efc75de7dc4fb2), and every decisive operand came back identical - the 2049-entry range again held 2048 tuples in 128 batches and returned in 0ms (events 30, 31, 32), the 2050-entry range again held 2049 tuples in 129 batches and did not return with three stack samples (events 33, 34, 35), and the default-configuration prefix again recorded staged commit index 12001 with snapshot index 0 and no snapshots listed against a bound of 8192, with the replay-enabled restart not returning and the control returning (events 36 to 41). The only differing operand was the control's return latency, 3ms in the first execution and 1ms in the repeat. A citation check of this account against the retained observations found no other discrepancy. Correction note: this successor extends the previous account of this question with the default-configuration receipt; sources, the earlier executions, knowledge uses, conclusions and limitations are otherwise unchanged, and the counterevidence item that recorded default-cadence reachability as unmeasured is replaced by the measured statement. Correction note: this successor differs from the previous account of this question only in two transcribed numbers and their attribution; the flag-off control of receipt 3d04552f58e548b0ae089192392b564f returned in 1ms and the same control of receipt e82594cc274340f0a961608cda519e00 returned in 5ms, as those receipts record, and the earlier account had them swapped. Everything else - sources, executions, knowledge uses, counterevidence, limitations and joint history - is unchanged. Mechanism. With Config.RestoreCommittedLogs set, NewRaft reads the persisted commit index and calls processLogs with it before starting any background goroutine (api.go:723-754). processLogs walks the committed range and appends a commit tuple only for entries prepareLog accepts - commands and barriers always, configuration entries when protocolVersion > 2, and nothing for no-op or deprecated peer entries - flushing a batch whenever the batch reaches config.MaxAppendEntries and delivering it through a select whose only other case is shutdownCh (raft.go:1296-1364 with prepareLog at raft.go:1364-1391). fsmMutateCh is created with capacity 128 (api.go:560) and its only consumer runFSM is started by api.go:636-638 after the replay returns, so a replay needing more than 128 delivered batches waits for a consumer that cannot exist until the blocked call finishes, with no timeout. Block and bound. Receipt 3d04552f58e548b0ae089192392b564f restarts a single-voter prefix holding 9001 committed entries above an absent snapshot (event 16, 141 delivered batches at MaxAppendEntries 64); the flag-on restart admitted by event 17 did not return within 8000ms (event 18, stack naming processLogs at raft.go:1346 called from restoreFromCommittedLogs at api.go:753 called from NewRaft) while the flag-off restart of the same shape returned in 1ms (event 21) and became leader (event 22); receipt e82594cc274340f0a961608cda519e00 reproduces both, with its own flag-off control returning in 5ms (its event 21) and adds a pair at MaxAppendEntries 16 where 1501 replayed entries (94 batches) returned in 0ms (event 25) and 3001 entries (188 batches) did not (event 29). Receipt 8a710c75d1814aafad5d24b536998539 then places the boundary exactly: at MaxAppendEntries 16 one prefix ends at staged commit index 2049, whose replayed range contains 2048 entries that become commit tuples, that is 128 delivered batches (event 31), and NewRaft returned in 0ms (event 32, comparison holds); the other ends at 2050 with 2049 tuples, that is 129 delivered batches (event 34), and NewRaft did not return within 8000ms (event 35, comparison violated, three stack mentions naming the replay frames). The pair differs only in one replayed command, so the bound is the 128-slot channel measured in delivered batches, and any count derived from the log's index range is an upper bound: the entry-based figure for the returning case also reads 129 batches (event 31), because the range includes entries that produce no tuple. Default configuration. Receipt 0cb30b6bf8874d479b83653bd4e6c553 builds two further single-voter prefixes with Config.DefaultConfig values left untouched: event 36 records SnapshotThreshold 8192, MaxAppendEntries 64, SnapshotInterval 120s, 12000 applied commands, staged commit index 12001, snapshot index 0, no snapshots listed, and a committed range of 12001 entries above the snapshot against the bound of 128*64 = 8192 tuples; the flag-on restart did not return within 8000ms with four stack samples naming the replay frames (event 38, comparison violated), while the flag-off control returned in 3ms (event 41, comparison holds). The trigger therefore needs no tuning of threshold or batch size: the automatic snapshot loop wakes on a randomized timeout of up to SnapshotInterval (snapshot.go:71-95), so a node that commits faster than that cadence carries a range above the bound into its restart. Availability. Receipt ebb3bb9fa26547f790129eea909d1d5a builds three voters at MaxAppendEntries 16, commits 3000 commands through a leader that is not A (event 9), restarts A in a goroutine, and observes the same non-return (event 10). With the replay on the group kept committing with two members live and lost the ability after one further member was stopped (event 11: commit_while_two_live_ok true, commit_after_stop_ok false, error 'leadership lost while committing log'), while with the replay off the same stop left the pair {leader, A} live and the command succeeded (events 13, 14). That pair reproduced identically in two later executions of the same harness (receipts fe822d82c6264158826e0303b4ce59b1 and 67fc59b525e54d01b709a19592689357, events 9 to 14 in each: staged commit index 3001 with the replay enabled and 3000 with it disabled, the replay-enabled restart not returning, the group committing with two members live and losing the ability after the second stop, and the control returning in 0ms and committing), so the availability consequence now rests on three executions with the same split, and a fourth at the library's default batch size: receipt 7ac7e4f67d194ad5b4e196c9b73f9897 records a prefix with 9000 committed commands and MaxAppendEntries 64 (event 15: staged commit index 9001, 141 batches by the entry-based count, snapshot index 0), the replay-enabled restart not returning with two stack samples naming the replay frames (event 16), the group committing while two members were live and losing that ability after the second stop (event 17, comparison violated). The consequence therefore does not depend on the smaller batch size used in the earlier cases. The corrected unit matters for the recorded gaps: the three correction gaps ask about the premise change, and the receipt answers them substantively - the returning case's entry-based figure was 129 batches while only 128 were delivered, so counts derived from a log index range are upper bounds. Defect account: with Config.RestoreCommittedLogs enabled, NewRaft cannot complete startup whenever the replay delivers more than 128 batches, that is more than 128*MaxAppendEntries entries that become commit tuples above the restored snapshot index; at the default MaxAppendEntries of 64 that is 8192 tuples, and it is proportionally smaller for any smaller legal value, as measured at 16 (2048 tuples returning, 2049 blocking). The failure is a startup liveness failure: the node never begins participating, so a single-voter group stays down, a larger group runs with proportionally less fault tolerance, and one further stop was enough to make a three-voter group unable to commit. Pending operations the leader had accepted are answered with ErrLeadershipLost once it steps down (raft.go:508-528). Reproducibility of the bound pair: receipt 962196ed81fd46cfb9476bbd63c2c637 is a second execution of the same fixed experiment and returns the same operands as receipt e82594cc274340f0a961608cda519e00 - events 23 and 25 record 1501 replayed entries with 94 batches and a return in 0ms, events 27 and 29 record 3001 entries with 188 batches and no return with the replay frames in the stack, and the only differing operand is the flag-off control's return latency, 5ms then 3ms. Boundary at a second batch size: the same harness now places the replay on both sides of the channel's capacity at the default MaxAppendEntries of 64. Receipt f394d8354fab48138079db19ca3deb13 records 8193 staged entries holding 8192 tuples in 128 delivered batches that returned in 2ms (events 36 to 38), and 8194 staged entries holding 8193 tuples in 129 delivered batches that did not return, with four stack samples naming the replay frames (events 39 to 41). With the 16-batch pair that puts the boundary at 128 delivered batches at two batch sizes, that is 128 * MaxAppendEntries tuples, 2048 and 8192 respectively, rather than a property of one setting.
